- `buildDailyPlan` scored only the first 12-hour horizon via `buildWeightedSlots`, but computed `confidenceFor(slots)` across all returned forecast slots (up to 24 hours). Added a regression where six sparse near-term hours and eighteen richer later hours must not elevate the 12-hour plan above medium confidence. Updated the test point helper to support hours beyond 23 without producing invalid timestamps.

### Bounded product-correctness fix
- `getNextChange` previously treated the first future forecast sample as the current rain state. If current conditions were dry but the first future hour had high precipitation probability, the summary could skip the first rain-start event; the old unit test called the forecast hour “wet now” without any current observation.
- The selector now accepts an optional current wet/dry baseline. `WeatherDecisionField` derives it only from a fresh current OpenWeather icon (rain/showers/thunderstorm); when current evidence is stale, it does not infer the baseline from stale current data. Invalid or out-of-range precipitation probabilities are unknown, never silently converted to dry.
- The selector returns a distinct `unavailable` state when no usable future samples survive filtering. The summary shows a localized unavailable state when hourly data is empty or the decision evidence has expired instead of claiming “no notable change” without usable evidence.
- Added domain and component regressions for current-dry → first-hour rain, current-wet → first-hour dry, invalid probability, empty hourly data, and expiry while the panel remains mounted. Added Turkish and English copy for the unavailable state.

- Dedicated branch/worktree: `automation/hava81-run11-20261024-0947-client-rate-limit` / `/home/chatgpt/hava81-autonomous-20261024-0406`, based on exact main `bf4f0da53022119ed0d8ffe1cfa645e3c4211aee`.
- The API now returns `503` plus normalized `Retry-After` for an upstream provider quota response and avoids backend retries, but the browser HTTP client previously retried every `503` using its generic bounded backoff. Because the client caps waits at 30 seconds, a provider `Retry-After: 120` could still cause another upstream-facing request too early. The client now parses the structured error code and does not automatically retry `PROVIDER_RATE_LIMITED`; ordinary transient 503 responses retain the existing retry behavior.
- Regression test proves a structured provider-rate-limit 503 with `Retry-After: 120` makes exactly one browser fetch even after advancing the fake clock beyond the 30-second generic retry cap. No weather values, cache policy, provider credentials, or manual retry affordances changed.
- Passed locally on this exact base: focused HTTP-client tests **30/30**, full frontend Vitest/coverage **1,024/1,024 across 106 files** (92.39% statements, 86.02% branches), frontend type-check, ESLint/CSS lint, production build (81 Turkish + 81 English province pages), and `git diff --check`. ### Bounded client error-contract fix
- `src/api/httpClient.ts` previously trusted four deterministic service-state codes (`PROVIDER_RATE_LIMITED`, `PROVIDER_CIRCUIT_OPEN`, `UPSTREAM_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`, `AIR_QUALITY_HOURLY_UNAVAILABLE`) on any HTTP error status. The API contract emits these codes with HTTP 503; if a malformed or mismatched response paired one with HTTP 500/404, it could suppress normal transient retry or misclassify a not-found response.
- Now only recognize these structured codes for HTTP 503. Other status codes retain their normal status-based mapping and retry policy. Added regressions proving a mismatched `PROVIDER_RATE_LIMITED` + HTTP 500 response still follows the bounded retry path and the same code + HTTP 404 maps to `NOT_FOUND` without retry. Actual structured 503 behavior and explicit user retry remain unchanged; no weather values, scoring, provider configuration, cache policy, or API runtime changed.

### Independent current-weather freshness fix
- Found a data-integrity gap in the current-weather BFF contract: `timestamp` is the provider sample time, while `meta.fetchedAt` is when the BFF fetched/cached the payload. The current BFF does not emit `meta.observedAt`. Previously, the frontend freshness helper fell back to `meta.fetchedAt`, so an old provider sample fetched moments ago could appear fresh.
- Current branch: `automation/hava81-run11-current-observation-meta-20261024-2152`, based on `e28ad60f3ef5f93395f87124a6c01bf78a546bb6` before the PR #1441 merge. It now normalizes `meta.observedAt` from the source `timestamp` when the optional metadata field is absent; rejects malformed, materially future, or inconsistent explicit observation timestamps; and applies the same source-time fallback to cached weather deserialization. The regression specifically covers a three-hour-old sample with a fresh `fetchedAt`, which must remain stale.
- Local validation before this checkpoint: focused API/hook/freshness tests **211/211**, full frontend Vitest **1,077/1,077 across 106 files**, TypeScript, ESLint/CSS lint, and `git diff --check` all passed. No API or production build/deploy was run.
- Read-only live audit: axe reported **0 violations** across Turkish/English, light/dark, desktop and mobile city/plan configurations; at 320px with 200% text there was no document-level horizontal overflow; the mobile search keyboard flow exposed suggestions and maintained active-descendant selection. The production provinces endpoint returned all **81/81** provinces with zero invalid/stale observations (observed ages within the freshness window). These checks did not modify production.

- **Change:** `http_get` now accepts optional `HAVA81_GITHUB_TOKEN`, but attaches it only to HTTPS requests with exact hostname `api.github.com`; Authorization is an unredirected header so it cannot be copied to a different host. Runbook/README require a repository-only fine-grained token with read-only Contents/Actions/Pull requests permissions. No interactive token or OpenWeather secret was copied into the service.
